O(\log\log n)
time and
O(m)+\overline{O}(n)
work w.p.
1-2/(\log n)^{2}
.

If
|V|\leq n^{0.1}
at the beginning of Stage
3
(which can be detected in
O(\log^{*}n)
time and
O(n)
work w.p.
1-n^{-9}
by approximate compaction), then in Step
1
of
SampleSolve
(G)
we remove all loops from
E
in
O(1)
time and
O(m)
work;
next, we remove all parallel edges from
E
by PRAM perfect hashing on all edges of
E
in
O(m)
work and
O(\log^{*}n)
time w.p.
1-n^{-9}
(
[
GMV91
]
).
This results in a simple graph with at most
|V|^{2}\leq n^{0.2}
edges, so the work of running the algorithm in Theorem
2
on this graph is at most
O(n^{0.2}\cdot\log n)\leq O(n)
w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{9}
, giving
O(m+n)
total work for Step
1
w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{8}
.
By Lemma
6.1
and the assumption on
\lambda
, we have
\lambda_{G}\geq(\log n)^{-10}
at the beginning of Step
1
.
Let
d
be the maximum diameter of any component in
G
, then
d\leq O(\log n/\lambda)\leq(\log n)^{12}
.
Removing loops and parallel edges from
G
does not affect the diameter of
G
, nor its components.
Therefore, the algorithm in Theorem
2
computes all connected components of
G
in
O(\log d+\log\log n)\leq O(\log\log n)
time w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{9}
.
Step
4
takes
O(n)
work and
O(1)
time.
As a result, if
|V|\leq n^{0.1}
at the beginning of Stage
3
, then Stage
3
takes
O(m+n)
work and
O(\log\log n)
time w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{7}
.

Suppose
|V|>n^{0.1}
at the beginning of Stage
3
.
By Lemma
5.25
, w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{5}
, we have
\deg(G)\geq b
at the beginning of Step
2
of
SampleSolve
(G)
.
By
|V|>n^{0.1}
and Corollary
C.3
, w.p.
1-n^{-0.1c}\geq 1-n^{-9}
(choosing
c
large enough), the spectral gap
\lambda
of any component
\mathcal{C}
in
G
and the spectral gap
\lambda^{\prime}
of this component
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
G^{\prime}
after sampling satisfy

\left|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\right|\leq C^{\prime}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\ln n}{(1/(\log n)^{7})\cdot\deg(G)}}\leq b^{-0.4}

by
1/(\log n)^{7}\cdot\deg(G)\geq b/(\log n)^{7}\geq C\ln n
and
b\geq(\log n)^{100}
.
So
\lambda^{\prime}\geq\lambda-b^{-0.4}\geq b^{-0.2}
by
\lambda\geq b^{-0.1}
from the previous paragraph.
Since
\lambda^{\prime}>0
, the component
\mathcal{C}
stays connected after sampling, and thus
V(\mathcal{C})=V(\mathcal{C^{\prime}})
.
Therefore, it remains to prove that each such component
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
contracts to
1
vertex in Step
3
of
SampleSolve
(G)
.
Let
d^{\prime}
be the diameter of component
\mathcal{C^{\prime}}
.
We have
d^{\prime}\leq O(\log n/\lambda^{\prime})\leq O(b^{0.2}\log n)\leq(\log n)^{30}
by
b=(\log n)^{100}
.
Therefore, the algorithm in Theorem
2
computes all connected components of
G^{\prime}
in
O(\log d^{\prime}+\log\log n)\leq O(\log\log n)
time w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{9}
, giving all connected components of
G
.
Now we compute the work. If
|E|\leq n^{0.2}
then Step
3
takes
O(n)
work w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{8}
as discussed in the previous paragraph.
Now suppose
|E|\geq n^{0.2}
.
By a Chernoff bound over all edges, after Step
2
of
SampleSolve
(G)
, the number of edges in
G^{\prime}
is at most
m/(\log n)^{6}
w.p.
1-n^{-9}
. So the algorithm in Theorem
2
takes
O(m+n)/(\log n)
work in Step
3
w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{9}
.
As a result, Stage
3
takes
O(m+n)
work and
O(\log\log n)
time w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{4}
for this case.

Summing up all works and running time over all
3
stages and by a union bound, the theorem follows.
∎

The total work of the algorithm will be improved to
O(m+n)
, and
the success probability will be boosted to
1-n^{-c}
for arbitrary constant
c>0
in §
8
.
The assumption on the component-wise spectral gap of the input graph will be removed in §
7
, resulting in a connected components algorithm for any given graph with any component-wise spectral gap
\lambda
over all components that runs in
O(\log(1/\lambda)+\log\log n)
time and
O(m)+\overline{O}(n)
work w.p.
1-n^{-c}
for arbitrary constant
c>0
.

7
The Overall Algorithm: Removing the Assumption on Spectral Gap

In this section, we remove the assumption that the component-wise spectral gap
\lambda
over all components of the input graph is at least
(\log n)^{-10}
, giving an algorithm that runs in
O(\log(1/\lambda)+\log\log n)
time.

7.1
Algorithmic Framework

The overall algorithm starts with running
Reduce
(V,E,k)
with
k=10^{6}\log\log n
on the input graph
(V,E)
for preprocessing (same as Stage
1
) such that the number of vertices in the current graph is at most
n/b^{10}
w.h.p., where
b=(\log n)^{100}
initially.
Next, we interweave the algorithms
Filter
from Stage
1
,
Increase
from Stage
2
, and
SampleSolve
from Stage
3
, such that in each
phase

i
(defined below in the pseudocode):

•

If the component-wise spectral gap
\lambda
(unknown to the algorithm) is at least
b^{-0.1}
, then
Increase
makes
\deg(G)\geq b
in
O(\log b)
time, and
SampleSolve
(with other subroutines) solves the problem in
O(\log b)
time. Otherwise,
\lambda<b^{-0.1}
.

•

Revert the state of algorithm
Filter
to the previous phase.
Update
b\leftarrow b^{1.1}
.
Run
O(1.1^{i}\log\log n)
rounds of
Filter
(and algorithm
Matching
with other subroutines) to reduce the number of vertices in the current graph to
n/b^{10}
.

Connectivity
(G=(V,E))
:

1.

Initialize the labeled digraph: for each vertex
v\in V
:
v.p=v
.

2.

Run
Reduce
(V,E,k=10^{6}\log\log n)
to obtain the current graph
G^{\prime}
.

3.

Sample each edge of
G^{\prime}
w.p.
1/(\log n)^{7}
independently to get random subgraph
H_{1}
. Sample each edge of
G^{\prime}
w.p.
1/(\log n)^{7}
independently to get random subgraph
H_{2}
. The sampled graphs are copied out so that
G^{\prime}
is unchanged.

4.

Copy the edges of
G^{\prime}
to create an edge set
E_{\text{filter}}
.

5.

For
phase

i
from
0
to
10\log\log n
:
E_{\text{filter}}=\textsc{Interweave}(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
, if
E_{\text{filter}}=\emptyset
then goto Step
6
.

6.

Shortcut
(V)
.

In the above,
H_{1}
is the edges
sampled to create the sparsified graph before we run
Theorem
2

[
LTZ20
]
,
and
H_{2}

is an independently sampled set of edges to help us create the skeleton
graph more work-efficiently in Stage
2
of the algorithm.
In particular, now that we need to
run the algorithm for each assumption of the component-wise
\lambda
,
we can no longer afford to spend
O(m)
work for each assumption –
for this reason, we sparsify the graph before creating
the skeleton graphs such that we can
create the skeleton graph in

O(m/\log^{2}n)
work.
See §
7.3

for more details.

We now describe the
Interweave
algorithm which essentially searches for the right spectral gap by squaring the guess in every iteration.

Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
:

1.

Set
b=(\log n)^{100\cdot 1.1^{i}}
.

2.

\textsc{Increase}(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
—
see §
7.3
for more details.

3.

Run
20\log b
rounds of
H_{1}=\textsc{Expand-Maxlink}(H_{1})
then run
10^{4}\log\log n
rounds of the algorithm in Theorem
2
on
H_{1}
.
Alter
(H_{1})
.

4.

If all edges in
E(H_{1})
are loops then call
Remain
(G^{\prime},H_{1})
and return
\emptyset
.

5.

Revert the labeled digraph and graph
H_{1}
to their states in Step
1
.

6.

Repeat for
10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}\log\log n
rounds:
Matching
(E_{\text{filter}})
,
Alter
(E_{\text{filter}})
, delete each edge from
E_{\text{filter}}
w.p.
10^{-4}
.

7.

Repeat for
i+2\log\log n
times:
Shortcut
(V(G^{\prime}))
.

8.

Edge set
E^{\prime}=\{(v,w)\in E(G^{\prime})\mid v.p\in V(G^{\prime})\backslash V(E_{\text{filter}})\}
.
Alter
(E^{\prime})
.

9.

Repeat for
10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}\log\log n
rounds:
Matching
(E^{\prime})
,
Shortcut
(V(G^{\prime}))
,
Alter
(E^{\prime})
.

10.

Reverse
(V(E_{\text{filter}}),E(H_{2}))
.

11.

Return
E_{\text{filter}}
.

Remain
(G^{\prime},H_{1})
:

1.

Alter
(E(G^{\prime}))
.

2.

Edge set
E_{\text{remain}}=E(G^{\prime})\backslash E(H_{1})
.

3.

Remove loops and parallel edges from
E_{\text{remain}}
.

4.

Compute the connected components of graph
(V(E_{\text{remain}}),E_{\text{remain}})
by Theorem
2
.

At the beginning of each phase
i
, the algorithm assumes that
\lambda\geq b^{-0.1}
where
b=(\log n)^{100\cdot 1.1^{i}}
.
If the assumption is correct, then the connected components of the input graph is correctly computed in
O(\log b)=O(\log(1/\lambda))
time.
Otherwise,
b
is updated to
b^{1.1}
and the number of vertices is reduces to
n/b^{10}
for the next phase under the assumption that
\lambda\geq b^{-0.1}
.

The algorithm eventually succeeds in a phase under the correct assumption.
Since the running time
O(\log b)
in each phase is geometrically increasing, the total running time is asymptotically the running time of the phase under the correct assumption, which is
O(\log(1/\lambda))
as desired.

An additional technique issue is to reduce the work in each phase from
O(m+n)
to
O(m+n)/\log n
such that the total work over all
O(\log\log n)
phases are
O(m+n)
, which will be presented later in this section:
In §
7.3
we reduce the work of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
by modifying the algorithm
Increase
(V,E,b)
in §
5.3
;
in §
7.4
we reduce the work of computing the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
8
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
.

7.2
Basic Properties of
Connectivity

We give some basic properties of the algorithm
Connectivity
, which will be used later.

Lemma 7.1
.

During the execution of
Connectivity
(G)
, at the beginning of each phase in Step
5
, all vertices in
V(G^{\prime})
are roots or children of roots.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.21
, after Step
2
, all trees are flat and edges in
E(G^{\prime})
are only adjacent to roots, which also holds for edge set
E_{\text{filter}}
(Step
4
).
By an induction on phase
i
from
0
to
10\log\log n
, it suffices to prove that for any phase, if at the beginning of the phase all vertices in
V(G^{\prime})
are roots or children of roots, then this still holds at the beginning of the next phase.
We do not consider the case of
E_{\text{filter}}=\emptyset
in Step
5
because there will be no next phase.
Consider the
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in this phase.
Step
2
to Step
5
can be ignored because
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
does not return
\emptyset
and thus the labeled digraph (as well as
H_{1}
) are the same as in Step
1
dues to Step
5
.
By an induction on rounds in Step
6
and Lemma
4.5
, the edges in
E_{\text{filter}}
are only adjacent to roots at the beginning of each round of Step
6
, and any vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
has distance at most
10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}\log\log n
from the root of the tree containing
v
because such distance increases by at most
1
in each round.
Each application of
Shortcut
(V(G^{\prime}))
in Step
7
reduces such distance by a factor of at least
3/2
if the distance is at least
2
.
Therefore, after
i+2\log\log n\geq\log_{3/2}(10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}\log\log n)
applications of
Shortcut
(V(G^{\prime}))
, all vertices in
V(G^{\prime})
are roots or children of roots after Step
7
.
The
Alter
(E^{\prime})
in Step
8
moves all edges only adjacent to roots since
E^{\prime}\subseteq E(G^{\prime})
.
Finally, by an induction on rounds in Step
9
and Lemma
4.5
, the edges in
E^{\prime}
are only adjacent to roots at the beginning of each round of Step
9
and all vertices in
V(G^{\prime})
are roots or children of roots at the beginning of each round of Step
9
dues to the
Shortcut
(V(G^{\prime}))
in each round.
This finishes the total induction and gives the lemma.
∎

Definition 7.2
.

Let graph
G^{\prime}
be computed in Step
2
of
Connectivity
(G)
.
Given a time
t
in the execution of
Connectivity
(G)
,
for each vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
,
define
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\coloneqq\left|\left\{(u,w)\in E(G^{\prime})\mid u.p=v\right\}\right|
,
and define graph
\overline{G^{\prime}}
as the graph
G^{\prime}
after executing
Alter
(E(G^{\prime}))
, where the parent points are given in the labeled digraph at time
t
.

In our later analysis, we will frequently use
\overline{G^{\prime}}
, because we cannot afford executing
Alter
(E(G^{\prime}))
as it would take
\Theta(m)
work.
Recall from §
4
that an active root is a vertex that is a root with adjacent edges to another root in the current graph.
Our current graph would be
G^{\prime}
if we executed
Alter
(E(G^{\prime}))
at the end of each phase, which is unaffordable, but we can still define the
current graph
to be
\overline{G^{\prime}}
to be consistent.
18
18

18

We will show how to detect active roots in desired work later in the proof of Lemma
7.8
.

As a result, a root
u
that is non-active must be in a tree containing all the vertices in the component of
u
in
G^{\prime}
, and thus
u
and all its descendants can be ignored in the rest of the execution of the algorithm because the computation of this component is finished.

Lemma 7.3
.

During the execution of
Connectivity
(G)
, w.p.
1-n^{-7}
, for any integer
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
, at the beginning of each phase
i
in Step
5
, the number of active roots in
V(G^{\prime})
is at most
n/b^{10}
, where
b=(\log n)^{100\cdot 1.1^{i}}
.

Proof.

The proof is by an induction on phase
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
.
At the beginning of phase
0
,
|V(G^{\prime})|\leq n/(\log n)^{1000}
guaranteed by Stage
1
.
Suppose the number of active roots is at most
n/b^{10}
at the beginning of phase
i
where
b=(\log n)^{100\cdot 1.1^{i}}
, we will show that the number of active roots decreases to
n/b^{11}
at the end of phase
i
, giving the lemma.

Consider the
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in phase
i
.
We shall ignore Step
2
to Step
4
in the analysis, because the labeled digraph is reverted to Step
1
(by Step
4
) if
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
does not return in Step
3
.

By the proof of Lemma
4.14
, each round in Step
6
decreases the number of roots in
V(E_{\text{filter}})
by a factor of
0.001
w.p.
1-n^{-9}
.
There are at most
(\log n)^{3}
rounds in Step
6
by
i\leq 10\log\log n
.
So by a union bound, w.p.
1-n^{-8}
, at the end of Step
6
we have the number of roots in
V(E_{\text{filter}})
is at most

n/b^{10}\cdot 0.999^{10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}\log\log n}\leq n/b^{12}.

After Step
8
, the edge set
E^{\prime}
contains all edges adjacent to all roots in
V(G^{\prime})\backslash V(E_{\text{filter}})
, because all vertices in
V(G^{\prime})
are roots or children of roots by Lemma
7.1
and
Alter
(E^{\prime})
moves all edges to roots.
By the proof of Lemma
4.24
, the number of roots such that the root has an adjacent edge to another root in
V(E^{\prime})
decreases by a factor of
0.001
w.p.
1-n^{-9}
in each round of Step
9
.
By a union bound over all the at most
(\log n)^{3}
rounds in Step
9
, the number of such roots (with an adjacent edge to another root) in
V(E^{\prime})
at the end of Step
9
is at most
n/b^{12}
w.p.
1-n^{-8}
.
By the same proof as in the proof of Lemma
4.24
,
the
Reverse
(V(E_{\text{filter}}),E(H_{2}))
in Step
10
makes the number of active roots at most
2n/b^{12}\leq n/b^{11}
w.p.
1-n^{-7}
.
∎

7.3
Reducing the Work of
Increase

In this section, we modify Step
1
and Step
10
in the
Increase
(V,E,b)
in §
5.3
such that the new
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
takes
(m+n)/(\log n)^{2}
work w.p.
1-2/(\log n)^{2}
.

Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
:

1.

Edge set
E_{H}=\textsc{SparseBuild}(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
, vertex set
V=V(G^{\prime})
, graph
H=(V(E_{H}),E_{H})
.

2.

Run Step
2
to Step
9
in the
Increase
(V,E,b)
in §
5.3
.

3.

Alter
(E(H_{1}))
.

7.3.1
Building Sparse Skeleton Graph

Recall that the
Build
(V,E,b)
in Step
1
of the
Increase
(V,E,b)
in §
5.3
takes
O(m+n)
work (Lemma
5.1
), because the sparse subgraph
H
is computed based on the input graph
(V,E)
with
n
vertices and
m
edges.
The computation of
H
relies on classifying vertices as high-degree and low-degree.
One of the key observation is that such classification is still accurate w.h.p. even if we sample the graph
\overline{G^{\prime}}
to get random subgraph
H_{2}
and classify vertices using edges of
H_{2}
: a high-degree vertex in
\overline{G^{\prime}}
is still high-degree w.h.p. in
H_{2}
(with different classification threshold).
The final subgraph
H
should also contain all edges adjacent to low-degree vertices.
Another key observation is that the number of edges adjacent to low-degree vertices is bounded, and we present a new technique to extract all these edges from the original input graph in
n/\poly(\log n)
work in §
7.4
.

SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
:

1.

For each active root
v\in V(G^{\prime})
: assign a block of
b^{9}
processors, which will be used as an indexed hash table
\mathcal{H}(v)
of size
b^{9}
.

2.

For each edge
(u,v)\in E(H_{2})
: write
(u,v)
into a random indexed cell of
\mathcal{H}(v)
.

3.

For each active root
v\in V(G^{\prime})
: if the number of items in
\mathcal{H}(v)
is more than
b^{8}
then mark
v
as
high
, else mark
v
as
low
.

4.

E^{\prime}=\{(u,v)\in E(G^{\prime})\mid u.p\text{~is~a~low~vertex}\}
.
Alter
(E^{\prime})
.

5.

Return
E^{\prime}\cup E(H_{2})
.

Note that in Step
2
of the
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
, we do not use hashing by vertex index as in
Build
(V,E,b)
.
This is because we cannot afford to removing parallel edges and loops in sublinear work as in
Build
(V,E,b)
.
As a result, we only obtain bounds on the vertex degrees (including parallel edges and loops) instead of bounds on the number of neighbors for each vertex as in §
5.1
.
We will show that under this case,
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
still makes every root high-degree.

Lemma 7.4
.

During the execution of
Connectivity
(G)
,
w.p.
1-n^{-9}
, for any vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
that is marked as high in any call to
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
,
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq b^{8}
in that call;
and for any vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
that is marked as low,
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\leq b^{9}
in that call.

Proof.

For any high vertex
v
, it must have degree at least
b^{8}
in graph
H_{2}
by Step
2
and Step
3
. Thus, the degree of
v
in
\overline{G^{\prime}}
(Definition
7.2
) is at least
b^{8}
since
H_{2}
is a subgraph of
\overline{G^{\prime}}
dues to the
Alter
(E(H_{2}))
within
Reverse
(V(E_{\text{filter}}),E(H_{2}))
in Step
9
of
Connectivity
(G)
and Lemma
7.1
, giving the first part of the lemma by Definition
7.2
.

A vertex
v
is low if at most
b^{8}
cells in
\mathcal{H}(v)
are occupied (Step
3
).
If
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq b^{9}
, then since each edge of
\overline{G^{\prime}}
is sampled into
H_{2}
w.p.
1/(\log n)^{7}
(by Step
3
of
Connectivity
(G)
, Definition
7.2
, and the fact that
\overline{G^{\prime}}
performs the same
Alter
as
H_{2}
by Step
9
of
Connectivity
(G)
and Lemma
7.1
), by a Chernoff bound, we get
\deg_{H_{2}}(v)\geq b^{9}/(\log n)^{8}\geq b^{8.5}
w.p.
1-n^{-11}
by
b\geq(\log n)^{100}
.
Then
v
is low w.p. at most

\binom{b^{8.5}}{b^{8}}\cdot\left(\frac{b^{8}}{b^{8.5}}\right)^{b^{9}}\leq b^{8.5b^{8}}\cdot b^{-0.5b^{9}}\leq b^{-b^{8}}\leq n^{-11},

where we used
b\geq(\log n)^{100}
. So, w.p.
1-n^{-10}
all such vertices are marked as high in one call to
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
by a union bound. During the execution of
Connectivity
(G)
,
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
is called for at most
10\log\log n
times (Step
5
of
Connectivity
(G)
).
So, all such vertices are marked as high w.p.
1-n^{-9}
in any call to
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
by a union bound, and we shall condition on this happening.
Since any vertex
v
with
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq b^{9}
is marked as high, we have that any low vertex
v
must have
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\leq b^{9}
. This gives the second part of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Let graph
H
be computed in Step
1
of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
.
W.p.
1-n^{-8}
, for any active root
v\in V(G^{\prime})
, either
\mathcal{C}_{\overline{G^{\prime}}}(v)=\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)
or
\sum_{w\in V(\mathcal{C}_{H}(v))}\deg_{H}(w)\geq b^{6}
.

Proof.

The first part of the proof is an analog of the proof of Lemma
5.4
.
Since the edge set
E_{H}
of graph
H
contains
E^{\prime}
, the set of edges adjacent to low vertices (Step
4
and Step
5
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
), we can run breath-first searches on
\mathcal{C}_{\overline{G^{\prime}}}(v)
and
\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)
simultaneously to get that if there is no high vertex in
\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)
, then
\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)=\mathcal{C}_{\overline{G^{\prime}}}(v)
.

On the other hand, if there is a high vertex
w
in
\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)
, then by Lemma
7.4
, w.p.
1-n^{-9}
,
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(w)\geq b^{8}
.
By a Chernoff bound, w.p.
1-n^{-9}
,
\deg_{H_{2}}(w)\geq b^{6}
because
H_{2}
is a random subgraph of
\overline{G^{\prime}}
with edge sampling probability
1/(\log n)^{7}
by Step
3
of
Connectivity
(G)
, Definition
7.2
, and the fact that
\overline{G^{\prime}}
performs the same
Alter
as
H_{2}
by Step
9
of
Connectivity
(G)
and Lemma
7.1
.
So, w.p.
1-n^{-8}
,
\deg_{H}(w)\geq b^{6}
since
E(H_{2})\subseteq E_{H}
(Step
5
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
),
giving the second part of the lemma.
∎

7.3.2
Increasing Vertex Degrees by Sparse Skeleton Graph

Lemma 7.6
.

Let graph
H
be computed in Step
1
of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
.
W.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{6}
,
for any component
\mathcal{C}
in
H
such that
\sum_{w\in V(\mathcal{C})}\deg_{H}(w)<b^{6}
, at the end of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
, all vertices in
\mathcal{C}
must be roots or children of roots and all edges in
E(H_{1})
adjacent to these vertices must be loops.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.5
, w.p.
1-n^{-8}
,
V(\mathcal{C})=V(\mathcal{C}_{\overline{G^{\prime}}})
, where
\mathcal{C}_{\overline{G^{\prime}}}
is the component in
\overline{G^{\prime}}
induced on vertex set
V(\mathcal{C})
.
Next, we prove that the number of vertices in
\mathcal{C}
is less than
b^{6}
.
If
\mathcal{C}
contains only
1
vertex then the claim holds.
Otherwise, since each
v\in V(\mathcal{C})
has at least
1
adjacent edge in
H
(otherwise
v
is a singleton), we get
|\mathcal{C}|<b^{6}
by
\sum_{w\in V(\mathcal{C})}\deg_{H}(w)<b^{6}
.
Therefore, for any shortest path
P
in
\mathcal{C}
, there are at most
b^{6}
vertices on
P
.

Using the same proof as of Lemma
5.24
, w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{7}
, at the end of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
, we obtain that all vertices in
\mathcal{C}
must be roots or children of roots and they must be in the same tree.
The
Alter
(E(H_{1}))
in Step
3
of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
moves all edges adjacent to vertices in
\mathcal{C}
to the root, which must be loops.
∎

By the same discussion after Lemma
5.24
, such small component (with total degree less than
b^{6}
) in
H
can be ignored in the rest of the entire algorithm as the connectivity computation on them is already finished.

Lemma 7.7
.

During the execution of
Connectivity
(G)
, w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{5}
, for any integer
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
and any active root
v\in V(G^{\prime})
at the end of Step
2
within
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
, it must be

\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq b=(\log n)^{100\cdot 1.1^{i}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.6
, w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{6}
, any vertex in a component of
H
with total degree less than
b^{6}
is ignored in the rest of the algorithm since the computation of its component is finished.
Therefore, we assume that any vertex
v\in V
is in a component with total degree at least
b^{6}
.

If any shortest path in
\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)
has less than
b^{6}
vertices on it, then since Step
2
of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
runs Step
2
to Step
9
in the
Increase
(V,E,b)
in §
5.3
, we can use the same proof in the second paragraph in the proof of Lemma
5.25
to get that w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{6}
, all vertices in
\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)
contracts to
v
.
So, the degree of any root
v\in V(G^{\prime})
has
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq b^{6}/2
because the total degree of
\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)
is at least
b^{6}
and all edges in
\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)
would be adjacent to
v
if we executed
Alter
(E(G^{\prime}))
(Definition
7.2
).

If there exists a shortest path in
\mathcal{C}_{H}(v)
with at least
b^{6}
vertices on it, then using the same proof in the proof of Lemma
5.25
for this case, we get that w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{6}
, the degree of
v
in
G^{\prime}
would be at least
b
if we executed
Alter
(E(G^{\prime}))
.
Using the same argument in the previous paragraph,
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq b
holds w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{6}
for this case.

By a union bound over all
10\log\log n
phases,
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq b
holds w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{5}
for all integers
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
and all roots
v\in V(G^{\prime})
.
∎

Lemma 7.8
.

If the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
4
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
can be computed in
n/(\log n)^{7}
work, then
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
takes
(m+n)/(\log n)^{2}
work w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{2}
.

Proof.

By a Chernoff bound, w.p.
1-n^{-9}
, we have
|E(H_{1})|\leq m/(\log n)^{6}
and
|E(H_{2})|\leq m/(\log n)^{6}
by Step
3
of
Connectivity
(G)
, and we shall condition on this happening.

In Step
1
of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
, consider the work of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
.
Detecting whether a vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
is an active root can be done by checking whether all of the following
3
conditions hold:
(i)
v=v.p
,
(ii) in the previous phase, at the end of Step
6
in
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
, any edge in
E_{\text{filter}}
does not connect
v
to another root,
(iii) in the previous phase, at the end of Step
9
in
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
, any edge in
E^{\prime}
does not connect
v
to another root.
Condition (i) can be checked by all vertices in
V(G^{\prime})
in
O(1)
time, taking
|V(G^{\prime})|\leq n/(\log n)^{10}
work.
Condition (ii) can be checked by adding
O(|E_{\text{filter}}|)
work into Step
6
in
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
then write a flag into the private memory of
v
, which does not change the asymptotic work of that step.
Condition (iii) can be checked by adding
O(|E^{\prime}|)
work into Step
9
in
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
then write a flag into the private memory of
v
, which does not change the asymptotic work of that step.
All in all, the total work (attributes to
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
) of letting each vertex know whether itself is an active root is
O(n/(\log n)^{10})
.

By Lemma
7.3
, w.p.
1-n^{-7}
, the number of active roots is at most
n/b^{10}
,
so Step
1
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
takes
O(n/b)\leq n/(\log n)^{9}
work (here we assume all active roots are in an index array of length at most
2n/b^{10}
, which is guaranteed by approximate compaction at the end of each phase, see Lemma
7.18
for details).
Step
2
takes
O(|E(H_{2})|)\leq O(m/(\log n)^{6})
work.
Step
3
takes
O(n/b^{10}\cdot b^{9}\cdot O(\log b^{9}))\leq n/(\log n)^{9}
work by a binary tree counting argument.
Computing edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
4
takes
n/(\log n)^{7}
work by the precondition of the lemma.
By Lemma
7.4
, w.p.
1-n^{-9}
, for any vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
that is marked as low,
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\leq b^{9}
, giving at most
b^{9}
edges in
E^{\prime}
adjacent on
v
by Definition
7.2
.
Since non-active roots can be ignored by the discussion after Definition
7.2
, and the number of active roots is at most
n/b^{10}
, we have that
|E^{\prime}|\leq n/b
w.p.
1-n^{-6}
.
So, the following
Alter
(E^{\prime})
takes
O(|E^{\prime}|)\leq O(n/b)
work.
Summing up, Step
1
of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
takes
O(m+n)/(\log n)^{6}
work.

Consider Step
2
of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
.
Firstly, we show that
|E(H)|\leq(m+n)/(\log n)^{5}
w.p.
1-n^{-8}
.
By the previous paragraph,
|E^{\prime}|\leq n/b
w.p.
1-n^{-6}
.
Since
|E(H_{2})|\leq m/(\log n)^{6}
, we have that
|E_{H}|=|E(H)|\leq(m+n)/(\log n)^{5}
w.p.
1-n^{-5}
by Step
5
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
.
Secondly, the vertex set
V
in Step
2
to Step
9
of
Increase
(V,E,b)
is replaced by the set of active roots, which is already described in the second paragraph of this proof and by running approximate compaction on all active roots at the end of each phase. By Lemma
7.3
, the number of active roots is at most
n/b^{10}
.
Since the requirements on
H
and
|V|
in §
5.3
are satisfied, (the proof of) Lemma
5.19
still holds.
In the
Increase
(V,E,b)
in §
5.3
, its Step
2
to Step
9
perform
(m+n)/(\log n)^{2.5}
work w.p.
1-2/(\log n)^{3}
(see footnote
14
).
As a result, Step
2
of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
takes
(m+n)/(\log n)^{2.5}
work w.p.
1-2/(\log n)^{3}
.

Step
3
of
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
takes
O(|E(H_{1})|)\leq O(m/(\log n)^{6})
work.
Summing up and by a union bound, the lemma follows.
∎

The computation of the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
4
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
will be given in §
7.4
.

7.4
Reducing the Work of Computing Edge Set
E^{\prime}

In this section, we present a new technique that work-efficiently computes

•

The edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
4
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
.

•

The edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
8
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
.

7.4.1
Building the Auxiliary Array

The computation of edges set
E^{\prime}
mentioned above relies on an auxiliary array that is computed and stored in the public RAM at the end of Stage
1
where the current graph is
G^{\prime}
(Step
2
of
Connectivity
(G)
).
We need the following tool.

Lemma 7.9

(Section 1.3 in
[
HR92
]
)
.

The
padded sort
is to sort an array of
m
items such that after sorting, the items are in sorted order in an array of length at most
2m
.
If the item values to be sorted are integers in
[m]
, then
m
items can be padded sorted in
O(\log\log m)
time and
O(m)
work w.p.
1-m^{-20}
.

In our application, we padded sort all
m
edges of
G^{\prime}
by their first ends (see below).
If
m\leq n^{0.3}
, then the non-singletons in
G^{\prime}
are at most
2n^{0.3}
, which can be detected in
O(n)
work.
Since we only need to compute the connected components of the non-singletons, one can call Theorem
2
for
\log n
times in parallel to compute the connected components of
G^{\prime}
in
O(\log d+\log\log n)\leq O(\log(1/\lambda)+\log\log n)
time and
O(m+2n^{0.3})\cdot\log n\cdot O(\log n)\leq O(n)
work w.p.
1-n^{-9}
.
Therefore, we assume
m\geq n^{0.3}
, which means the padded sort succeeds w.p. at least
1-n^{-6}
.
Meanwhile, the padded sort takes
O(\log\log n)
time by
m\leq n^{c}
.

BuildAuxiliary
(G^{\prime})
:

1.

Padded sort all edges of
G^{\prime}
by their first ends and store the output array
\mathcal{A}
.

2.

For each item
(v,w)\in\mathcal{A}
in parallel: if the first end of the predecessor of
(v,w)
in
\mathcal{A}
is not
v
then write the index of
(v,w)
in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
into
v.l
in the private memory of (the processor corresponding to)
v
.

3.

For each item
(v,w)\in\mathcal{A}
in parallel: if the first end of the successor of
(v,w)
in
\mathcal{A}
is not
v
then write the index of
(v,w)
in
\mathcal{A}
into
v.r
in the private memory of
v
.

4.

For each vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
: if
v.r-v.l\geq(\log n)^{90}
then (approximate) compact the subarray from index
v.l
to index
v.r
(inclusive) in
\mathcal{A}
.

Padded sorting (Step
1
) create empty cells between items.
In Step
4
, if the subarray from index
v.l
to
v.r
satisfies
v.r-v.l\geq(\log n)^{90}
, then the non-empty cells are moved to the beginning of this subarray while the original subarray is erased.
The array
\mathcal{A}
is called the
auxiliary array
that is stored in the public RAM and will be used to efficiently compute the edge set
E^{\prime}
during the algorithm.
Each cell of
\mathcal{A}
that contains an edge
e
also stores the processor id corresponding to
e
for the purpose of mapping each edge in
\mathcal{A}
back to the corresponding edge processor, which can be done by copying the edge set and storing the corresponding processor id in the item (edge) to be sorted before padded sorting.

Lemma 7.10
.

BuildAuxiliary
(G^{\prime})
takes
O(\log\log n)
time and
O(m)
work w.p.
1-n^{-5}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.9
and the discussion after it, Step
1
takes
O(m)
work and
O(\log\log n)
time w.p.
1-n^{-6}
. Since
\mathcal{A}
is indexed, we assign a processor for each of its cell.
Step
2
and Step
3
take
O(m)
work and
O(1)
time by
|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2m
.
Step
4
takes
O(\log^{*}n)
time and
O(v.r-v.l)
work w.p.
1-1/2^{(\log n)^{90/25}}\geq 1-n^{-9}
for each
v\in V(G^{\prime})
by Lemma
4.2
.
W.p.
1-n^{-8}
, the total work of Step
4
is at most
\sum_{v\in V(G^{\prime})}O(v.r-v.l)=O(|\mathcal{A}|)\leq O(m)
by telescoping.
The lemma follows.
∎

We will use the following property of the auxiliary array
\mathcal{A}
.

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be constructed in
BuildAuxiliary
(G^{\prime})
.
W.p.
1-n^{-4}
, for any vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
, all edges adjacent on
v
in
E(G^{\prime})
are stored in the subarray of
\mathcal{A}
that starts from index
v.l
and ends at index
v.l+v.s
, where
v.s\leq\max\{(\log n)^{90},2\deg_{G^{\prime}}(v)\}
.

Proof.

By the proof of Lemma
7.10
, w.p.
1-n^{-5}
, the padded sorting in Step
1
and all approximate compactions in Step
4
succeed.
For any vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
, if
v.r-v.l<(\log n)^{90}
, then all adjacent edges are stored in the subarray of
\mathcal{A}
that starts from index
v.l
and ends at index
v.r\leq v.l+(\log n)^{90}
, so the lemma holds for this case.
Else if
v.r-v.l\geq(\log n)^{90}
, the all the
\deg_{G^{\prime}}(v)
edges adjacent to
v
are compacted to the subarray of
\mathcal{A}
that starts from index
v.l
and ends at index
v.r\leq v.l+v.s\leq v.l+2\deg_{G^{\prime}}(v)
by Definition
4.1
, and the lemma holds for this case.
∎

7.4.2
Computing the Edge Set in
SparseBuild

In this section we show that the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
4
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
can be computed in
n/(\log n)^{7}
work and
O(\log b)
time, giving the precondition of Lemma
7.8
.

Lemma 7.12
.

At the beginning of Step
4
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
, w.p.
1-n^{-9}
, for any vertex
u\in V(G^{\prime})
, if
u.p
is a low vertex, then
\deg_{G^{\prime}}(u)\leq b^{9}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.4
, w.p.
1-n^{-9}
,
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(u.p)\leq b^{9}
, and we shall condition on this happening.
Assume for contradiction that
\deg_{G^{\prime}}(u)>b^{9}
, then the degree of
u.p
is more than
b^{9}
if we perform
Alter
(E(G^{\prime}))
, giving
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(u.p)>b^{9}
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 7.13
.

W.p.
1-n^{-3}
, the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
4
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
can be computed in
O(\log b)
time and
n/(\log n)^{7}
work.

Proof.

In Step
4
of
SparseBuild
(G^{\prime},H_{2},b)
, each vertex
u\in V(G^{\prime})
checks whether
u.p
is a low vertex using
O(|V(G^{\prime})|)\leq O(n/(\log n)^{10})
work in total.
If
u.p
is low, then we do the following.
The processor corresponding to
u
awakens the processor corresponding to the cell indexed at
u.l
of the auxiliary array
\mathcal{A}
in round
0
, then in the next round (round
1
) the processor corresponding to the cell at
u.l
awakens the cells at
u.l
and
u.l+1
, then in round
2
, the processor at
u.l
awakens
u.l
and
u.l+1
, and the processor at
u.l+1
awakens
u.l+2
and
u.l+3
, and so on. That is, in round
j
, each of the
2^{j-1}
awakened processor at
u.l+k
awakens the processors at
u.l+2k
and
u.l+2k+1
.
We continue this process until the
(9\log b+1)
-th round or a cell containing an edge not starting at
u
is awakened.
Since in each round, a processor only needs to awaken
2
processors, the running time for this part is
O(\log b)
.
After
9\log b+1
rounds, all cells indexed from
u.l
to
u.l+2^{9\log b+1}=u.l+2b^{9}
such that the cell contains an edge starting at
u
are awakened by
u
(if a cell containing an edge not starting at
u
is awakened by
u
, we mark this cell as not awakened by
u
to avoid the case that a cell is awakened by more than
1
vertices).

By Lemma
7.12
, w.p.
1-n^{-9}
,
\deg_{G^{\prime}}(u)\leq b^{9}
if
u.p
is a low vertex.
By Lemma
7.11
, w.p.
1-n^{-4}
, all edges adjacent on
u
in
E(G^{\prime})
are stored in the subarray of
\mathcal{A}
that starts from index
u.l
and ends at index
u.l+u.s
, where

u.s\leq\max\{(\log n)^{90},2\deg_{G^{\prime}}(v)\}\leq 2b^{9}

by the previous sentence and
b\geq(\log n)^{100}
.
Therefore, if
u.p
is a low vertex, then w.p.
1-n^{-3}
, any cell that stores an edge in
E(G^{\prime})
adjacent to
u
is awakened by
u
.
The processor corresponding to each cell then notifies the stored edge (processor) that it is in
E^{\prime}
, giving the edge set
E^{\prime}
.

The total running time is
O(\log b)
.
The total work is at most
O(|V(G^{\prime})|+O(|E^{\prime}|))\leq n/(\log n)^{7}
because at most
2|E^{\prime}|
cells are awakened in total and
|E^{\prime}|\leq n/b
w.p.
1-n^{-6}
by the third paragraph in the proof of Lemma
7.8
.
The lemma follows.
∎

7.4.3
Computing the Edge Set in
Interweave

In this section we show that the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
8
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
can be computed in
n/(\log n)^{7}
work and
O(\log b)
time.

Lemma 7.14
.

During the execution of
Connectivity
(G)
, w.p.
1-n^{-7}
, for any phase
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
and for any vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
, if
\deg_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq(\log n)^{10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}}
, then
v.p\notin V(G^{\prime})\backslash V(E_{\text{filter}})
in Step
8
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
.

Proof.

Since the statement in the lemma is about the algorithm at Step
8
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
, we can ignore Step
2
to Step
4
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in this proof, because if the algorithm executes to Step
8
, then the labeled digraph is reverted to its state in Step
1
(by Step
4
).

In Step
4
of
Connectivity
(G)
, the edge set of
G^{\prime}
is copied as
E_{\text{filter}}
.
In each phase, the edges in
E_{\text{filter}}
are altered and deleted randomly in Step
6
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
. For each edge
e\in E_{\text{filter}}
at a timestamp of the algorithm, let the
corresponding edge

\overline{e}
of
e
be the edge in
E(G^{\prime})
that is altered (in Step
6
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
) over phases to become
e
at this timestamp, and let
e
be the
corresponded edge
of
\overline{e}
.

Let
v
be a vertex in
V(G^{\prime})
with
\deg_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq(\log n)^{10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}}
. At the end of Step
6
in phase
i
, consider the edge set
\mathcal{E}_{v}
of corresponded edges of all edges in
E(G^{\prime})
adjacent to
v
.
For each edge
\overline{e}\in\mathcal{E}_{v}
, every time the algorithm executes Step
6
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in a phase
j\leq i
, the edge
\overline{e}
is deleted w.p.
10^{-4}
.
In all the phases before and including phase
i
, the algorithm executes Step
6
for at most

\sum_{j=0}^{i}10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{j}\log\log n\leq 10^{8}\log\log n\cdot 1.1^{i}

times, so the probability that edge
\overline{e}
ever gets deleted before phase
i
is at most

1-(1-10^{-4})^{10^{8}\log\log n\cdot 1.1^{i}}.

Since
|\mathcal{E}_{v}|=\deg_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq(\log n)^{10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}}
, the probability that all edges in
\mathcal{E}_{v}
are deleted before phase
i
is at most

\left(1-(1-10^{-4})^{10^{8}\log\log n\cdot 1.1^{i}}\right)^{(\log n)^{10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}}}\leq\left(1-(\log n)^{-10^{5}\cdot 1.1^{i}}\right)^{(\log n)^{10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{e}\right)^{(\log n)^{9}}\leq n^{-9}.

Therefore, w.p.
1-n^{-9}
, there is an edge in
\mathcal{E}_{v}
that is not deleted at the beginning of Step
8
in phase
i
.
By the definition of corresponded edges, there is an edge in
E_{\text{filter}}
that is adjacent to
v.p
at this timestamp, which means
v.p\in V(E_{\text{filter}})
and thus
v.p\notin V(G^{\prime})\backslash V(E_{\text{filter}})
.
The lemma follows by a union bound over all the at most
10\log\log n
phases and all the at most
n
vertices.
∎

Lemma 7.15
.

W.p.
1-n^{-8}
, the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
8
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in any phase
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
of
Connectivity
(G)
satisfies
|E^{\prime}|\leq n/(\log n)^{9}
.

Proof.

Note that the definition of edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
8
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in phase
i
is exactly the edge set
E^{\prime}
at the end of Step
2
of
Extract
(E,k)
if we set
k=10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}\log\log n
, because
V(E_{\text{filter}})
is exactly the vertex set
V^{\prime}
defined at the end of Step
2
of
Extract
(E,k)
.

Using the same technique used in the proof of Lemma
4.15
, we get that the expected contribution to
|E^{\prime}|
from each vertex in
V(E_{\text{filter}})
is at most
2
,
and we get that
\E[|E^{\prime}|]\leq O(|V(E_{\text{filter}})|)
.
Using the technique in §
8
, we get that w.p.
1-n^{-9}
,
|E^{\prime}|\leq O(|V(E_{\text{filter}})|)\leq O(|V(G^{\prime})|)\leq n/(\log n)^{9}
.
The lemma follows from a union bound over all phases.
∎

With the above two lemmas, we are ready to give the cost of compute the edge set
E^{\prime}
.

Lemma 7.16
.

W.p.
1-n^{-3}
, the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
8
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in any phase
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
of
Connectivity
(G)
can be computed in
O(\log b)
time and
n/(\log n)^{7}
work.

Proof.

We shall use the auxiliary array
\mathcal{A}
built in §
7.4.1
, similar to the proof of Lemma
7.13
.
In Step
8
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
, each vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
first checks whether
v.p\in V(G^{\prime})\backslash V(E_{\text{filter}})
. Since each vertex knows whether itself its in
V(E_{\text{filter}})
in
O(1)
time by adding
O(|E_{\text{filter}}|)
work to Step
6
(which does not affect the asymptotic work of Step
6
), the above computation can be done in
O(|V(G^{\prime})|)\leq n/(\log n)^{10}
work and
O(1)
time.
Next, each vertex
v
such that
v.p\in V(G^{\prime})\backslash V(E_{\text{filter}})
awakens all its adjacent edges in
\mathcal{A}
following the same manner in the proof of Lemma
7.13
for
10^{4}\log b
rounds or until a cell containing an edge not starting at
v
is reached.
By Lemma
7.14
, w.p.
1-n^{-7}
, any such vertex
v
has
\deg_{G^{\prime}}(v)\leq(\log n)^{10^{6}\cdot 1.1^{i}}\leq b^{10^{4}}
by
b=(\log n)^{100\cdot 1.1^{i}}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
7.11
, w.p.
1-n^{-4}
, all adjacent edges of any such vertex
v
are awakened in
10^{4}\log b
rounds, and each awakened edge notifies the edge processor that it is in
E^{\prime}
.

The total running time is
O(\log b)
.
The total work is at most
n/(\log n)^{7}
because at most
2|E^{\prime}|\leq 2n/(\log n)^{9}
cells are awakened w.p.
1-n^{-8}
by Lemma
7.15
.
∎

7.5
The Work of
Connectivity

In this section, we give the total work of
Connectivity
(G)
.

Lemma 7.17
.

For any graph
G
, w.p.
1-1/\log n
,
Connectivity
(G)
takes
O(m)+\overline{O}(n)
work.

Proof.

Step
1
takes
O(n)
work.
By Lemma
4.25
, w.p.
1-n^{-2}
, Step
2
takes
O(m)+\overline{O}(n)
work because the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
4
of
Reduce
(V,E,k)
satisfies
\E[|E^{\prime}|]\leq\overline{O}(n)
by the same proof of Lemma
4.15
.
Step
3
and Step
4
take
O(m)
work.
Step
6
takes
O(n)
work.

Consider Step
5
.
Consider the
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in each phase
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
.
By Lemma
7.8
and Lemma
7.13
,
the
Increase
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
in Step
2
takes
(m+n)/(\log n)^{2}
work w.p.
1-2/(\log n)^{2}
.
By the same proof for Lemma
5.10
, w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{3}
, Step
3
takes
(m+n)/(\log n)^{3}
work, because
|E(H_{1})|
is bounded same as
|E(H)|
required in
Densify
(H,b)
.
The following
Alter
(H_{1})
takes
O(|E(H_{1})|)\leq m/(\log n)^{6}
work w.p.
1-n^{-9}
.
Step
4
without calling
Remain
(G^{\prime},H_{1})
takes the same work as in the previous step.
To implement Step
5
, we make a copy of
H_{1}
at the beginning of Step
1
, which takes
O(|E(H_{1})|)\leq m/(\log n)^{6}
work w.p.
1-n^{-9}
. To revert the labeled digraph, we copy all parent pointers for each vertex
v\in V(G^{\prime})
in Step
1
which takes
O(|V(G^{\prime})|)\leq n/(\log n)^{9}
work, because the vertices in
V(G)\backslash V(G^{\prime})
are non-roots after Step
2
of
Connectivity
(G)
which do not change their parent until Step
6
of
Connectivity
(G)
.
Running approximate compaction on all active roots (required by
\textsc{Increase}(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
when assigning blocks to active roots) at the end of each phase takes at most
n/(\log n)^{7}
work.
As a result, the total work of all steps but Step
6
to Step
10
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in all phases except the call to
Remain
(G^{\prime},H_{1})
is at most
2(m+n)/(\log n)^{2}\cdot 10\log\log n\leq(m+n)/\log n
w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{1.5}
.

Next, we show that the total work of Step
6
to Step
10
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
over all phases is at most
O(m+n)
w.p.
1-n^{-2}
.
To total work of Step
6
over all phases is
O(m)
w.p.
1-n^{-8}
.
Because the algorithm deletes each edge from
E_{\text{filter}}
with constant probability in each round of Step
6
over phases so
|E_{\text{filter}}|
decreases by a constant fraction in each round w.p.
1-n^{-9}
, and the claim follows from a union bound over all the at most
(\log n)^{4}
rounds.
To total work of Step
7
over all phases is
O(n)
by
|V(G^{\prime})|\leq n/(\log n)^{1000}
.
By Lemma
7.15
and Lemma
7.16
, the edge set
E^{\prime}
in Step
8
in any phase
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
satisfies
|E^{\prime}|\leq n/(\log n)^{9}
and Step
8
takes
n/(\log n)^{7}
work w.p.
1-n^{-3}
, so the total work of Step
8
and Step
9
over all phases is at most
O(1.1^{10\log\log n}\log\log n)\cdot n/(\log n)^{9}+n/(\log n)^{7}\leq O(n)
w.p.
1-n^{-3}
.
Step
10
in each phase takes
O(|V(E_{\text{filter}})|+|E(H_{2})|)
work, which is at most
O(|V(G^{\prime})|+n/(\log n)^{6})\leq n/(\log n)
w.p.
1-n^{-8}
, so the total work of Step
10
over all phases is
O(n)
w.p.
1-n^{-7}
.

Finally, we show that the call to
Remain
(G^{\prime},H_{1})
in Step
4
takes
O(m+n)
work w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{8}
, which completes the proof because it is called for
1
time during the execution of
Connectivity
(G)
.
Step
1
and Step
2
of
Remain
(G^{\prime},H_{1})
take
O(|E(G^{\prime})|)\leq O(m)
work.
Step
3
take
O(m)
work w.p.
1-n^{-9}
by PRAM perfect hashing
[
GMV91
]
.
We claim that at the beginning of Step
4
,
|E_{\text{remain}}|\leq n/(\log n)^{6}
w.p.
1-n^{-9}
.
Since the algorithm runs
Alter
(E(G^{\prime}))
in Step
1
, the graph
H_{1}
is an (unbiased) random subgraph of
G^{\prime}
by sampling each edge w.p.
1/(\log n)^{7}
independently.
The key observation is that, although
H_{1}
has been used (in Step
2
to Step
5
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
) before the current phase, it is reverted to its initial state (in Step
5
of the previous phase), so the execution of the algorithm before phase
i
(excluding Step
2
to Step
5
in each previous phase, which is irrelevant to the analysis of the current phase) is independent of the randomness in generating
H_{1}
.
19
19

19

Without this observation, it could be the case that the execution of the algorithm before the current phase is dependent on the generation of
H_{1}
, such that
H_{1}
is not an unbiased random subgraph of
\overline{G^{\prime}}
given the fact that the algorithm does not terminate before the current phase.

By the sampling lemma of Karger, Klein and Tarjan (
[
KKT95
]
), the number of edges in
G^{\prime}
connecting different connected components of its random subgraph
H_{1}
is at most
O(|V(G^{\prime})|/p)
w.p.
1-n^{-9}
where the edge sampling probability
p=(1/(\log n)^{7})
.
20
20

20

[
KKT95
]
states a more general version for the application in minimum spanning tree and the bound on the number of inter-component edges is in expectation. A high-probability result is a straightforward corollary (e.g., see
[
HZ96
]
, Lemma 9.1).

Therefore,
|E_{\text{remain}}|\leq n/(\log n)^{6}
w.p.
1-n^{-9}
at the beginning of Step
4
.
By Theorem
2
, Step
4
takes at most
n/(\log n)^{6}\cdot O(\log n)
work w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{9}
.
∎

7.6
The Running Time and Correctness of
Connectivity

In this section, we give the running time of
Connectivity
(G)
and prove that it correctly computes the connected components of the input graph
G
.

Lemma 7.18
.

During the execution of
Connectivity
(G)
, w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{2}
, for any integer
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
, phase
i
in Step
5
takes
O(\log b)
time unless
E_{\text{filter}}=\emptyset
, where
b=(\log n)^{100\cdot 1.1^{i}}
.

Proof.

Consider the
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
in phase
i
.
Consider the
\textsc{Increase}(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
in Step
2
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
.
In Step
1
of
\textsc{Increase}(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},b)
, we calculate the running time of

.
We already described how to let each vertex in

knows whether itself is an active root in the proof of Lemma

, so at the end of each phase the algorithm runs approximate compaction on all active roots to compact them in an index array of length
O(n/b^{10})
in

time w.p.
1-n^{-6}
by Lemma

.
Step

of

takes
O(1)
time since the active roots are indexed.
Step

takes
O(1)
time.
Step

takes
O(\log b)
time by the binary tree counting argument.
Step

takes
O(\log b)
time w.p.
1-n^{-3}
by Lemma

.
Therefore, Step

of

.
By the fourth paragraph in the proof of Lemma

and Lemma

.

.)
Summing up,

time w.p.

.

Consider Step

of

(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
.
By Lemma

, each

.
So all

by

. The

(E(H_{1}))
takes
O(1)
time.
Summing up, Step

of

time w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{7}
.

In Step

Each round of Step

.
Step

Summing up and by a union bound, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 7.19
.

During the execution of

(G)

21

The algorithm needs to run

By the first paragraph, the algorithm computes all connected components of

Lemma 7.20
.

For any graph
G
with component-wise spectral gap
\lambda
, w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{4}
,
Connectivity
(G)
computes all the connected components of
G
before or in phase
i
, where
i\in[0,10\log\log n)
is the minimum integer that satisfies
(\log n)^{-10\cdot 1.1^{i}}\leq\lambda
.

Proof.

First of all, such an integer
i
must exist because
(\log n)^{-10\cdot 1.1^{9c\log\log n}}\leq n^{-9c}
, and
\lambda\geq\phi^{2}/2\geq m^{-2}/2\geq n^{-3c}
by Cheeger’s inequality and
m\leq n^{c}
where constant
c\geq 2
.

By Lemma
7.19
, w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{8}
, if
Connectivity
(G)
terminates in a phase
j\in[0,10\log\log n)
, then it correctly computes all connected components of
G
.
Suppose
Connectivity
(G)
does not terminate before phase
i
.
Consider phase
i
and let
b=(\log n)^{100\cdot 1.1^{i}}
.
By Lemma
7.7
, w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{5}
, it must be
\overline{\deg}_{G^{\prime}}(v)\geq b
for any active root
v\in V(G^{\prime})
at the end of Step
2
within
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
.
By Definition
7.2
and Step
3
of
Connectivity
(G)
, at the end of Step
2
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
, the graph
H_{1}
is an (unbiased) random subgraph of
\overline{G^{\prime}}
by sampling each edge w.p.
1/(\log n)^{7}
independently (see the observation in last paragraph in the proof of Lemma
7.17
and footnote
19
).
Note that the component-wise spectral gap of
\overline{G^{\prime}}
is at least
\lambda
since the algorithm only performs contractions on
G
(same as in §
6
).
Now since
\deg(\overline{G^{\prime}})\geq b
and the sampling probability
p=1/(\log n)^{7}
, we have that
p\cdot\deg(\overline{G^{\prime}})\geq C\ln n
and thus Corollary
C.3
is applicable. We have that w.p.
1-n^{-9}
, the component-wise spectral gap
\lambda^{\prime}
of
H_{1}
satisfies

\lambda^{\prime}\geq\lambda-C^{\prime}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\ln n}{1/(\log n)^{7}\cdot b}}\geq b^{-0.1}-b^{-0.4}\geq b^{-0.2}

by
\lambda\geq(\log n)^{-10\cdot 1.1^{i}}=b^{-0.1}
, where
C^{\prime}
is the constant in Corollary
C.3
.
So the maximum diameter
d^{\prime}
over all components of
H_{1}
is at most
O(\log n/\lambda^{\prime})\leq b^{0.3}
.
Since any shortest path of
H
has at most
b^{0.3}+1\leq b^{7}
vertices, we can apply the same proof of Lemma
5.17
to get that
20\log b
rounds of
H_{1}=\textsc{Expand-Maxlink}(H_{1})
(Step
3
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
) contracts
H_{1}
such that its diameter becomes at most
1
w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{8}
, and the following call to Theorem
2
computes all connected components of
H_{1}
in
O(\log\log n)
time w.p.
1-1/(\log n)^{9}
(the same, more detailed proof appears in
[
LTZ20
]
).
As a result, the following
Alter
(H_{1})
makes all edges in
E(H_{1})
loops and Step
4
of
Interweave
(G^{\prime},H_{1},H_{2},E_{\text{filter}},i)
returns
\emptyset
.
So phase
i
is the last phase and
Connectivity
(G)
correctly computes all connected components of
G
by the second paragraph of this proof.
The lemma follows from a union bound.
∎

Lemma 7.21
.

For any graph
G
with component-wise spectral gap

,

Proof.

By Lemma

, w.p.

, Step

of

time.
By Lemma

, the
BuildAuxiliary
(G^{\prime})
at the end of Step

takes

time w.p.
1-n^{-5}
(required for reducing the work, see §

).
Step

, Step

, and Step

take
O(1)
time.
W.p.

, assuming Lemma

, Lemma

, and Lemma

hold.

Let phase
i

(G)
.
If

of phase

, before calling

(G^{\prime},H_{1})
in its Step

.
Now suppose

By Lemma

, it must be

\lambda\leq(\log n)^{-10\cdot 1.1^{i+1}}.

(6)

By Lemma

(G)
before calling

where the last inequality is by Equation (

).

The success probability
1-1/\log n
will be boosted to
1-n^{-c}
for arbitrary constant

in §
